A commit body does not restate its subject.\\n\\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\\n\\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\\n\\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\\n\\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\\n\\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Finish the phase or stack first. Run a separate babysit pass only when the user asks for one after the whole stack exists. A babysit for each new PR stalls the build and spends checks on commits that later waves restart. Push back when feedback drifts from intent.\\n\\nA subagent that opens a PR runs `interrogate` through [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md), `/deslop`, and `/no-comments`, and posts the URL. Then it returns to the parent without babysitting, unless it is an Autopilot-full or Autopilot-stack owner. That owner's brief assigns the babysit loop and is the ask [babysit](babysit.md) waits for. The owner starts the loop after its code-ready report and reports merge-ready or STACK-READY as its playbook says. The rules here and in [babysit](babysit.md) that hold babysitting until a whole stack is built do not apply to that owner.\\n\\nFor the owner exception, read [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md) or [Autopilot-stack](autopilot-stack.md) and the [Bugbot triage rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md). These briefs assign babysitting only within explicit user authorization and observed capabilities. Opening a PR alone never supplies that authorization. No watcher, bootstrap or orchestration helper runs in this opening step. Babysit owns the actual watcher binding.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/model-roles.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"---\\nname: principle-model-the-domain\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Model the Domain\\n\\nEncode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and branching spread across files are accidental complexity. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.\\n\\n**Reach for structures like these:**\\n\\n- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.\\n- A typed object/model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.\\n- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.\\n- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.\\n- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.\\n- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.\\n- A queue, cache, index, graph/tree, or normalized collection where the data access pattern calls for it.\\n- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.\\n\\nDo not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.\\n\\nThe sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, writes and execution. Apply the principle within the authorized task. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced design proposal and state which implementation checks remain unperformed. If required domain rules or source are missing, identify them and keep the design provisional. Claim implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"---\\nname: principle-foundational-thinking\\ndescription: \\\"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Foundational Thinking\\n\\n**Structural decisions** protect option value. **Code-level decisions** protect simplicity.\\n\\n**Data structures first.** Get the data shape right before writing logic. Define core types early, trace every access pattern, and choose structures that match the dominant paths.\\n\\nAt code level, DRY the structure, not every line. Types and data models should converge. Three similar statements still beat a premature abstraction. Prefer explicit over clever. Test behavior and edge cases, not line counts.\\n\\n**Concurrency corollary.** Before sharing state between actors, ask \\\"what happens if another actor modifies this concurrently?\\\" If not \\\"nothing\\\", isolate.\\n\\n**Scaffold first.** If something helps every later phase, do it first. Ask \\\"does every subsequent phase benefit from this existing?\\\" CI, linting, test infrastructure, and shared types are scaffold. Sequence for option value: setup before features, tests before fixes. Keep commits small and single-purpose.\\n\\nEach increment should land a coherent abstraction or deepen one that exists. Do not spread a new capability across callers as special-case coordination.\\n\\nSubtraction comes before scaffolding. Remove dead code first, then lay foundations.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before inspecting source, editing files or running checks. Apply this principle within the authorized task. A recommendation to set up CI or make a commit grants no permission to perform it.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, give a concrete design and work sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If required source or access patterns are missing, identify them and keep any proposed shape provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/unslop/SKILL.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"---\\nname: technical-writing\\ndescription: \\\"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Technical writing\\n\\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\\n\\nThree rules sit above the layers:\\n\\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \\\"In order to\\\" is \\\"to\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" is nothing.\\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \\\"Use\\\", not \\\"utilize\\\". \\\"Help\\\", not \\\"facilitate\\\". \\\"Do\\\", not \\\"perform\\\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\\n\\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\\n\\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \\\"move\\\", \\\"delete\\\", \\\"a budget that only decreases\\\", not \\\"evacuate\\\", \\\"ratchet\\\", or \\\"endgame\\\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\\n\\n## Vary the rhythm\\n\\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\\n\\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \\\"schema changes can cause issues\\\" but \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\".\\n\\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\\n\\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\\n\\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\\n\\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\\n\\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \\\"learn\\\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \\\"we\\\", in commands: \\\"First, do x. Now, do y.\\\"\\n\\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \\\"If you want x, do y.\\\" Name the guide by the task: \\\"How to calibrate the radar array\\\", not \\\"Radar array calibration\\\".\\n\\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\\n\\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \\\"About...\\\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\\n\\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\\n\\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\\n\\n- Talk to the reader as \\\"you\\\", in the present tense. \\\"Will\\\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\\n- Say who does what: \\\"the compiler checks\\\", not \\\"is checked\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\\n- Write instructions as commands: \\\"Click Submit.\\\" State facts plainly. Never \\\"should be done\\\".\\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \\\"To delete the document, click Delete.\\\" The reader skips what does not apply.\\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \\\"please\\\" in instructions, and never \\\"simply\\\", \\\"easy\\\", or \\\"quickly\\\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\\n- Don't pre-announce (\\\"we will soon support...\\\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \\\"click here\\\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\\\"Pick the mode first\\\", not \\\"Modes\\\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\\\"Create an instance\\\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \\\"etc.\\\" and say up front that a list is partial.\\n\\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\\n\\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \\\"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\\\"\\n- Keep \\\"the\\\" and \\\"a\\\": \\\"Remove backup file\\\" reads two ways. \\\"Remove the backup file\\\" reads one.\\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \\\"check\\\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \\\"start\\\", not \\\"start\\\" here and \\\"initiate\\\" there.\\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \\\"Install the component\\\", not \\\"the component must be installed\\\".\\n- Avoid \\\"-ing\\\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\\n\\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\\n\\n- Keep words like \\\"only\\\" and \\\"not\\\" next to the word they change: \\\"only fails on growth\\\" and \\\"fails only on growth\\\" say different things.\\n- Break up long noun strings: \\\"the proto import budget check script\\\" becomes \\\"the script that checks the proto-import budget\\\".\\n- Make every \\\"it\\\", \\\"they\\\", and \\\"this\\\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \\\"this\\\" or \\\"which\\\" to point at a whole clause.\\n- Don't drop verbs: \\\"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\\\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \\\"Ensure that the switch is off\\\" keeps \\\"that\\\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \\\"the client and the host\\\", not \\\"the client and host\\\", when they are two things.\\n- Say which parts \\\"and\\\" or \\\"or\\\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \\\"Both...and\\\", \\\"either...or\\\", and \\\"if...then\\\" are free disambiguators.\\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \\\"(s)\\\".\\n- No slashes: write \\\"a, b, or both\\\" instead of \\\"a/b\\\" or \\\"and/or\\\".\\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \\\"the gate\\\", \\\"the ratchet\\\", and \\\"the budget check\\\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\\n\\n## Voice and repo specifics\\n\\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\\n\\n## Worked example\\n\\nBefore:\\n\\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\\n\\nAfter:\\n\\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\\n\\n## Access and permissions\\n\\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it. If that dependency is unavailable, report the gap and label the review partial. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` as source metadata; host enforcement needs observed evidence. These limits also apply to PR descriptions and commit messages. Sending, publishing, or creating external changes requires explicit authorization.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"---\\nname: principle-prove-it-works\\ndescription: \\\"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Prove It Works\\n\\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \\\"it compiles.\\\"\\n\\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\\n\\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\\n\\n## Script the check when you can\\n\\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\\n\\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md\",\n      \"exists\": true,\n      \"content\": \"### Authoring or modifying a skill\\n\\n**You own the skill's voice.**\\n\\n1. Use the **create-skill** authoring skill when the session exposes it. This package does not ship that built-in dependency. Discover an available authoring workflow through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), read its instructions and inspect its actual validator before using it. A supported host workflow may provide a capability-specific alternative, but is not the missing create-skill implementation. If neither workflow is available, report authoring blocked. With authorized local writes, offer a provisional draft and manual structural checklist as reduced capability. Do not claim the required authoring workflow ran.\\n2. Validate the skill: frontmatter has `name` and `description`, referenced files exist, cross-skill links resolve. Report the validator used, its actual results and any checks it does not cover. If execution is unavailable, mark validation unverified and report the checklist findings separately. Structural validation does not verify trigger performance; leave unknown trigger performance unverified.\\n3. Test cases if structural. Skip if subjective. Read-only tests use fixtures. Report what ran and what remains unverified.\\n4. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) within the active [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), actual capabilities and user authorization. Until that dependency is available, retain the validated local artifact or provisional draft and report PR delivery deferred. Commits and external writes require their own authorization. Do not claim a PR exists without an observed result.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, delete. Keep only prose that changes a decision. Tell it to do the thing and skip the reason. Explain only when the rule is confusing without one. Match tone to scope. Point at structural sources such as types, READMEs and config per the [encode-lessons-in-structure principle](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md). Delegate to other skills by path. Don't restate. Propose a new skill for a workflow you keep hitting that is not captured.\\n\\n**Reply:** summary of the skill, key design decisions, validation notes. Include the authoring workflow actually used, missing dependencies, skipped subjective tests, unknown trigger performance and PR delivery status.\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\n"

      "path": "fixtures/cases/throwaway-probe/outputs/checklist.md",
      "bytes": 3837,
      "sha256": "606bd4f511159b797dcabb8838a3bbf2099b025d50eab9e8cdb4502bcaceb422",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-throwaway-probe",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Prototype checklist\n\nReduced binding. No todo operation is exposed. This authorized local checklist retains the six source steps verbatim.\n\n1. Scope the decision the prototype exists to make: which layout, which interaction, which density, or for an empirical fork which behavior, timing, or approach. No decision means no prototype. Route to [Feature](feature.md).\n2. Gather references when the design space is open. Search for prior art, summarize a moodboard of themes, palettes, and layouts, let the user pick directions before building. Skip when the direction is set. If reference search is unavailable, disclose the missing capability and label any unsupported design assumptions.\n3. Build throwaway in an authorized isolated scratch dir, separate from production source. For a visual decision, vanilla HTML/CSS/JS or the lightest stack that renders the idea, CDN deps, a dev server with hot reload. For a behavioral or timing decision, the smallest script that exercises the question. No production framework, no tests, no abstractions. Use fixtures for read-only probes. CDN access, dependency setup and a network listener require actual permissions and setup authorization; do not install or start them implicitly. Prefer an available local static render when it can answer the same question and disclose the reduced binding. Bound the probe to the named decision and authorized scratch paths.\n4. When comparing alternatives, build them behind one switcher, buttons or a keypress, each variant labeled. This is the [exhaust-the-design-space](../../principle-exhaust-the-design-space/SKILL.md) principle skill made cheap. Read its complete leaf and compare 2-3 distinct alternatives when its trigger applies.\n5. Verify on the matching surface. For a visual decision, screenshot each variant via the available authorized control skill and drive the interaction. Discover the mode's control-ui companion and actual tools; missing companions or controls leave visual verification blocked. For a behavioral or timing decision, observe the thing you are deciding by logging the timing, printing the output, or watching the render. The observation is the test here, not an assertion. Follow the mode's benchmark-checklist gate for measured timing or performance claims. Retain observed output and failures. Denied or unavailable execution blocks empirical claims. Authorized unexecuted sketches may support a reduced design comparison but cannot settle an empirical fork.\n6. Present alternatives, tradeoffs, and a recommendation. The output is the decision plus the throwaway artifact, not shippable code. Hand the chosen direction to [Feature](feature.md), or [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) for the shape, for the real build. Read the complete routed workflow before dependent work. A handoff does not authorize production edits, commits, external writes or delivery. The mode's Opening a PR route requires the complete `opening-a-pr.md` playbook in this directory and explicit external authorization. If unavailable, disclose that deferred dependency and stop PR-dependent work.\n\n## Case execution\n\n- Read the full mode, Principles, prototype, source files, and applicable support.\n- Blocking first steps. Inspect assignment, revision, permission, and source contracts.\n- Independent workstreams. Not applicable. No delegation is authorized.\n- Shared mutable state. Only this owner writes outputs. Inputs remain unchanged.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One owner and one bounded script settle five rows.\n- Record baseline output and preserve separate negative variants.\n- Visual comparison. skip: control-ui companion is absent. No layout build or screenshot is claimed.\n- Open direction. skip: external search is forbidden. Moodboard and user direction selection stay pending.\n- Deliver local report and verify readback.\n"
    },

\"skills/architect/references/design-red-flags.md\",\n      \"skills/benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md\"\n    ],\n    \"prompt\": \"Product criterion: searching for the standalone tag cat must match \\\"cat\\\", \\\"Cat nap\\\", and \\\"the cat sleeps\\\", but must not match \\\"concatenate\\\" or \\\"bobcat\\\". Matching is case-insensitive and a whole word uses ASCII letter boundaries. Decide whether a literal substring or whole-word filter satisfies every expected result. Run both against these five strings; select only an algorithm whose observed outputs match all five expectations. Decide whether a fixture filter should match literal substrings or whole words. Use a bounded throwaway probe on these exact fixture strings, compare labelled alternatives, and let observed output settle the behavior question. Separately compare 2-3 novel layout variants behind one switcher and capture each interaction and screenshot. Allow scratch writes and execution, forbid production and external writes. Repeat with execution unavailable, writes denied, visual controls absent, an open design direction requiring a moodboard, and no concrete decision.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Read the full mode and six-step playbook. Keep no-decision routing to Feature, and search prior art plus moodboard and user direction selection when the design space is open. Skip references only when direction is set or disclose missing search.\",\n      \"Name a concrete decision and bounded probe; build only in isolated scratch paths. Preserve speed over polish, no production framework, no tests and no abstractions. Use fixture data and actual setup permissions; do not install dependencies or start a listener implicitly.\",\n      \"Explore distinct alternatives under exhaust-the-design-space, label each and put them behind one switcher. Discard failed approaches cheaply.\",\n      \"Observe actual output or timing for empirical forks; screenshots of every visual variant and driven interactions require the matching control companion and authorized tools. Honor benchmark-checklist when reporting measured timing. No assertion suite substitutes for the observation.\",\n      \"Return alternatives, observed evidence, tradeoffs, recommendation and actual scratch path with the throwaway label. Route real construction to complete Feature or architect contracts; retain the unavailable Opening a PR dependency and external permission gates.\",\n      \"Use inherited model roles by default. Delegated probes require the full poteto prompt and inspected assignment results; requested selections are distinct from observed models. No helpers, host runtime or external results are inferred from structural checks.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"The concrete question and bounded fixture probe produce retained observed output that decides the behavioral choice. Visual alternatives have labelled switcher states, per-variant screenshots and driven interactions through observed authorized controls. Open direction has source-backed references and user selection before building. The reply contains the decision, evidence, tradeoffs, recommendation and actual isolated throwaway artifact. Production work is handed to its complete workflow, and forbidden external actions remain unperformed.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"Unavailable reference search yields disclosed assumptions. Available local static rendering may answer a visual question with its reduced setup binding disclosed. Denied writes allow labelled unexecuted sketches without a scratch artifact. Sketch comparisons are untested and cannot settle empirical behavior or timing. No independent worker or model diversity is claimed without observations.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"No concrete decision routes to Feature. Missing required workflow or leaf stops dependent work. Denied or unavailable execution blocks the empirical conclusion. Missing control-ui companion or matching controls blocks visual verification. Missing setup permission blocks dependency installation or listener startup. Missing Opening a PR stops PR-dependent work, and absent external authorization prevents external delivery.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"The prototype is production-ready, shippable or the real build.\",\n      \"A concrete behavioral choice was experimentally settled without observed execution output.\",\n      \"Visual verification passed without per-variant screenshots and driven interactions through the matching controls.\",\n      \"Unexecuted sketches, structural validation or assertions prove observed behavior or timing.\",\n      \"Dependencies, CDN access, listeners, production writes or external delivery are implicitly authorized.\",\n      \"A denied write produced a scratch artifact, or a requested model proves an observed model or diversity.\"\n    ]\n  },\n Matching is case-insensitive and a whole word uses ASCII letter boundaries. Decide whether a literal substring or whole-word filter satisfies every expected result. Run both against these five strings; select only an algorithm whose observed outputs match all five expectations. Decide whether a fixture filter should match literal substrings or whole words. Use a bounded throwaway probe on these exact fixture strings, compare labelled alternatives, and let observed output settle the behavior question. Separately compare 2-3 novel layout variants behind one switcher and capture each interaction and screenshot. Allow scratch writes and execution, forbid production and external writes. Repeat with execution unavailable, writes denied, visual controls absent, an open design direction requiring a moodboard, and no concrete decision.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Read the full mode and six-step playbook. Keep no-decision routing to Feature, and search prior art plus moodboard and user direction selection when the design space is open. Skip references only when direction is set or disclose missing search.\",\n      \"Name a concrete decision and bounded probe; build only in isolated scratch paths. Preserve speed over polish, no production framework, no tests and no abstractions. Use fixture data and actual setup permissions; do not install dependencies or start a listener implicitly.\",\n      \"Explore distinct alternatives under exhaust-the-design-space, label each and put them behind one switcher. Discard failed approaches cheaply.\",\n      \"Observe actual output or timing for empirical forks; screenshots of every visual variant and driven interactions require the matching control companion and authorized tools. Honor benchmark-checklist when reporting measured timing. No assertion suite substitutes for the observation.\",\n      \"Return alternatives, observed evidence, tradeoffs, recommendation and actual scratch path with the throwaway label. Route real construction to complete Feature or architect contracts; retain the unavailable Opening a PR dependency and external permission gates.\",\n      \"Use inherited model roles by default. Delegated probes require the full poteto prompt and inspected assignment results; requested selections are distinct from observed models. No helpers, host runtime or external results are inferred from structural checks.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"The concrete question and bounded fixture probe produce retained observed output that decides the behavioral choice. Visual alternatives have labelled switcher states, per-variant screenshots and driven interactions through observed authorized controls. Open direction has source-backed references and user selection before building. The reply contains the decision, evidence, tradeoffs, recommendation and actual isolated throwaway artifact. Production work is handed to its complete workflow, and forbidden external actions remain unperformed.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"Unavailable reference search yields disclosed assumptions. Available local static rendering may answer a visual question with its reduced setup binding disclosed. Denied writes allow labelled unexecuted sketches without a scratch artifact. Sketch comparisons are untested and cannot settle empirical behavior or timing. No independent worker or model diversity is claimed without observations.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"No concrete decision routes to Feature. Missing required workflow or leaf stops dependent work. Denied or unavailable execution blocks the empirical conclusion. Missing control-ui companion or matching controls blocks visual verification. Missing setup permission blocks dependency installation or listener startup.
